IMPERIALISM II PHONE REBUILD — IMPLEMENTATION AUDIT
Updated: 2026-11-09

STATUS: DEVELOPMENT BUILD, NOT A COMPLETE PLAYABLE CAMPAIGN.
The rejected outputs/IMP-2-Phone prototype is not the implementation base.
The new standalone implementation is game/. No original executable, hotseat
installation, or PC save has been changed. There is no Windows wrapper in
the phone runtime. Offline native-code interpretation is used only by audit
scripts to recover data and verify numerical calculations.

SOURCE AUDIT
- Historical Euro1500 maps: checked all 18,712 land pixels; 1,178 terrain
  tiles; 83 provinces; one town per province. Original map hashes recorded
  in outputs/Scenario-audit.json and game/data/europe.json.
- 114 original technology entries (7 discoveries, 107 research technologies)
  from English strings. All prerequisite slots and research divisions now
  extracted from original technology initializer 0x639150. Ten differences
  from the manual transcription are recorded in Native-research-audit.json.
- Native manufacturing, recruitment and construction cost records extracted.
  Native regiment and ship statistics extracted into native-tables.json.
- Native research gain 0x638710 independently exercised in an offline,
  isolated interpreter with only known helper responses: 59 numerical cases.
  JavaScript curve matches these within 1e-7. Funding costs are division x
  $100 per level. Printing Press sets efficiency to 122%, later Industrial
  Funding to 150%. Native completion threshold is cumulative progress >0.5;
  progress increments vary uniformly from zero to twice expected gain.
  Rebuild random sequencing differs from native seeded sequencing; project
  carry-over and cancellation details still need further native audit.

IMPLEMENTED
- Local-scale historical Europe tile map and strategic zoom, touch controls.
- Separate builder, engineer and explorer; timed development, road building,
  ports, prospecting and deployment; construction consumes materials.
- Resource-level transport graph, primitive/improved road throughput,
  port shipping allocations and cargo limits, delivery ledger.
- Workforce food/luxury consumption, recruitment, training, labour allocation
  and manufacturing using warehouse stock. Lost provinces break transport.
- Technology prerequisite selection, goals, funding, variable progress,
  estimated turns, three/four slots, known/available technology views.
- Recruitment costs and tech gates for civilians, regiments and ships.
- Save/export/import of the development scenario; no PC save interaction.
- Basic economic opponent planner using the same commands and stock rules.

TESTED

- Explicit development -> roads -> delivery -> manufacturing chain, missing
  connections, interrupted roads, sea cargo, workforce food/luxury limits,
  training and native recruitment costs, technology graph and native curve.
- Forty-turn England economy regression reaches 1580 with ten workers,
  continuing iron/timber deliveries, and no starvation. This is NOT evidence
  of campaign completion or strong military AI.
- Headless Chrome: widths 320/375/412/540, five panels, funding controls,
  production, recruitment, turn advancement and save reload pass.
- Browser screenshots under work/rebuild-v2/. Local map screenshot inspected.

TACTICAL SOURCE AUDIT — 2026-11-09
- Located actual installed-build routines through runtime class names, rather
  than trusting the differently built shipped .map symbol addresses.

  Thirty native regiment cases matched by standalone JavaScript.
- TArmyTacUnit ApplyDamage: 0x6212b0. Physical HP at +4, effective fighting
  strength at +0x40; state +0x1c becomes 1 when effective strength reaches zero,
  or 3 when physical HP reaches zero. Nine isolated native reference cases.
- game/tactical.mjs implements these primitives. They are NOT yet connected to
  campaign attacks. Manual-backed medal/effective-strength scaling is separate;
  the native attack formula and experience conversion still require audit.
- Test suite now 48/48 passing. No new full battle or campaign claim.

  work/rebuild/tactical_reference.py. Original executable hash recorded there.

NATIVE ATTACK UPDATE — 2026-11-09
- Actual installed-build FireOn identified at 0x61c5d0; LaFireOn at 0x61cec0.
- Offline reference script fire_reference.py exercised 360 open-field cases:
  every extracted regiment type, half/full health, 0/2/4 medals, melee/ranged.
  Geometry, no fort crossing, and empty general lists were explicit stubs;
  the damage arithmetic and integer conversion executed from original bytes.
- game/tactical.mjs openFieldDamage matches every captured physical/effective
  loss pair. Category vulnerability is not uniform: cavalry ranged loss and
  artillery melee loss differ. Native float stores/truncation reproduced.
- IMPORTANT: native FireOn uses tactical field +4 for attack strength, while
  +0x40 is independently reduced by the effective-loss argument. Do not claim
  the manual's green-bar wording proves the exact field mapping. Full combat
  state/health-bar audit remains needed before integrating a complete battle.
- Native fort protection table at 0x711d08 is [1,.8,.7,.6,.5,.5], but the mapping
  between tactical wall codes and strategic fort levels has not been verified.
  Do not treat these raw codes as an established fort-level percentage table.
- Full suite: 49 passing tests. New combat primitives remain isolated from
  campaign attacks; tactical terrain, walls, opportunity fire, counterattacks,
  generals and battlefield controls are not yet a complete playable system.

TACTICAL GRID UPDATE — 2026-11-09
- game/battlefield.mjs provides hex neighbours and graph distance. Every cell
  in the original 29 x 16 tactical indexing grid matches the native neighbour

  wall rules, UI, combat AI and campaign resolution remain to connect.

BATTLE DEPLOYMENT CONTROLLER — 2026-11-09
- game/battle.mjs now creates separate, serializable battle state from supplied
  campaign regiments and tactical cells. Individual deploy/reposition/undeploy
  commands retain health, ID and experience; do not mutate campaign inputs.
- Enforces side ownership, legal deployment cells, occupancy and 10-regiment
  cap (12 with Nationalism, plus best general medals). Excess units remain
  explicit reserves. Medals are supplied separately from campaign XP.
- Both sides must finish deployment before phase changes to combat, attacker
  first. Invalid commands are atomic; deployment survives JSON roundtrip.
- Full suite 56 passing tests. This controller is NOT yet wired into campaign
  attacks or the UI, and combat-phase commands are still outstanding. It is
  a foundation for the requested battle system, not a finished skirmish mode.

OPEN-FIELD ATTACK INTEGRATION — 2026-11-09
- Low-level attackOpenField now applies the native-verified damage calculations
  to battle regiments, records casualties and clears destroyed-unit occupancy.
- Native FireAndCycle 0x61c0e0 establishes sequential melee retaliation:
  defender replies after being hit, unless broken or destroyed. Reply therefore
  uses surviving health. Ranged fire does not create a melee reply.
- Tests cover this sequencing, shot consumption, range and hostile ownership,
  and rejecting unsupported fort/terrain/general cases without mutation.
- 59 total tests pass. No full battle claim: attack operation remains internal;
  turn controller, opportunity fire, movement, terrain, forts and UI are pending.

OPEN-FIELD TURN LOOP — 2026-11-09
- Combat commands now enforce the active side and available unit, support
  normal-ground path movement, done, attack, end turn and retreat outcomes.
- Reserved-fire handling follows manual p.61: unused enemy shots during
  movement against threats, and remaining shots offered at end of the opposing
  turn. Threat reach and target choice are rebuild policies needing native
  parity validation; they are not certified copies of original tactical AI.
- Native-verified damage, sequential melee counterattacks and casualty state
  feed the loop. Reserves survive and do not count as deployed combatants.
- An integration test deploys both sides, advances them, fights multiple melee
  turns and reaches an outcome while serializing/restoring each turn.
- 62 tests pass. This is not full campaign/battle completion. Fort/terrain
  modifiers, zones of control, fleeing/surrender/capture, generals, full AI,
  battle UI and campaign resolution are still missing. Controller remains
  unexposed to campaign attacks until these gaps are addressed.

ARTILLERY MOVEMENT / FIRING — 2026-11-09
- Manual p.54 distinguishes heavy artillery (move OR shoot) from light
  artillery (move AND shoot). The active-turn command now enforces this for
  all four heavy and all three light artillery types from the native table.
- Movement is recorded in serializable battle state and reset on the next
  friendly turn. Invalid move-then-fire commands leave state untouched.
- Opportunity target eligibility now excludes moved heavy guns, since they
  cannot attack on that turn. End-turn fire can still target them (manual p.61).
- Reserved fire after moving is retained under p.61's general unused-shot rule;
  this interpretation has controller tests but awaits native execution parity.
- 64/64 full-suite tests pass. Battle UI, fortified combat and campaign
  integration remain unfinished; this does not establish full playability.

GENERAL RALLY — 2026-11-09
- Located native RallyUnit at 0x61e8e0. Isolated execution captured 364
  reference cases, with controlled neighbour list and RNG and UI/cycle stubs.
  Evidence: outputs/Native-rally-audit.json (includes original executable hash).
- Standing troops regain floor(physical health / 10) * (general medals + 3),
  capped at surviving physical health. Broken troops rally with probability
  (medals + 5) * 10 percent, regaining min(health, floor(health/10) + 20).
  No casualties are healed. Enemy neighbours are unaffected.
- Implemented deterministic rally primitive and battle command for adjacent
  allied regiments, in native neighbour order. General's action finishes.
  Rebuild seeded RNG is saved with battle state; not original RNG sequence.
- 66/66 tests pass, including all native rally cases and command ownership,
  adjacency, action exhaustion, casualty preservation and save reproducibility.
- General damage-reduction modifiers still need native reference coverage;
  the existing general-combat guard stays in place until that is implemented.
  No battle UI/campaign integration or full-playability claim yet.

GENERAL MORALE PROTECTION — 2026-11-09
- Audited FireOn's defender general-list arithmetic with 72 reference cases:
  medals 0..4, melee/ranged, inactive generals and different list orders.
  Evidence: outputs/Native-general-fire-audit.json and reproducible harness
  work/rebuild/general_fire_reference.py (iterator storage is explicitly stubbed).
- Active deployed generals reduce effective-strength loss, not physical loss.
  Best general supplies float32(2 - medals*0.2 - 0.2) instead of the default
  effective-loss multiplier 2. Multiple generals do not stack.
- Removed the general-combat rejection from the open-field battle controller.
  Each strike checks the target side's currently active deployed generals, so
  broken or reserve generals provide no protection, including on retaliation.
- Integration checks attack -> casualty/morale differences -> turn handover ->
  save/load -> adjacent rally, preserving casualties. 68/68 tests pass.
- Still pending: fort/terrain combat, fleeing/capture, battle UI/AI, campaign
  attack/invasion resolution and campaign completion. No full-game claim.

BROKEN-REGIMENT SURRENDER AUDIT — 2026-11-09
- Located FleeUnit at 0x61a790 and MakeRetreatMap at 0x61fb90.
- Captured 144 original FleeUnit decisions with explicit retreat-map, movement,
  enemy-list and RNG stubs. Covers blocked/moving units, nearby/distant enemy,
  six regiment categories and random draws. No UI or installed files changed.
- Implemented fleesOrSurrenders primitive: blocked broken troops surrender;
  after movement, active enemies within distance <3 contribute firepower*10*hp,
  capped at three times the fleeing unit's corresponding strength. The native
  random remainder is compared strictly against that own strength.
- Tests match all 144 reference cases. This is NOT yet called by the controller:
  retreat path generation/selection, fleeing timing, map exits and casualty
  reports must be integrated together. Existing early battle-outcome logic
  remains provisional and must not resolve campaign survivors yet.
- MakeRetreatMap evidence: seeds the side's exit column (left 0 for attacker,
  right activeWidth-1 for defender), then propagates through unoccupied tiles,
  excluding terrain 4 and intact wall cells except defender gates. Native
  FleeUnit chooses the lowest retreat-distance reachable cell with randomized
  tie breaking. Preserve this structure in the next implementation step.
- Full suite: 69 passing tests. Fort battles, capture, UI and campaign warfare
  remain unfinished. No full-game completion claim.

OPEN-FIELD ROUTING INTEGRATION — 2026-11-09
- Added exit-distance propagation following open-field MakeRetreatMap structure:
  correct side exit, occupied-cell barriers, impassable terrain 4, BFS distances.
  Fort routes explicitly rejected until gates/walls are integrated.
- Broken regiments now automatically select a reachable retreat destination at
  their side's turn start, with saved seeded tie breaking. They either escape
  with surviving hp, continue fleeing, or use the audited surrender calculation.
  Surrender clears occupancy and marks a distinct surrendered flag; escape is
  state 2 plus escaped flag. Undeployed reserves are retained separately.
- Outcome checking now counts deployed broken regiments as present. It no
  longer declares victory immediately when their morale reaches zero.
- Tests cover multi-turn escape, surrounding blockers, map barriers, and full
  combat through actual retreat/outcome with save/load between turns.
- 72/72 full-suite tests pass. Exact original unit-cycle timing and movement
  path parity remain pending; retreat uses existing open-field move allowances.
  Voluntary whole-army retreat still has provisional immediate resolution.
  Artillery capture, forts, battle UI, campaign warfare and Android delivery
  remain unfinished. This is not a full playable recreation yet.

TACTICAL OPPONENT — 2026-11-09
- Added game/battle-ai.mjs: one legal action at a time, suitable for interruptible
  autoplay or AI turns. Never acts during the human side's turn or after finish.
- Deploys within native zones/caps, keeps reserves, prioritizes generals and
  stronger regiments, positions melee/ranged units differently. In combat it
  rallies adjacent damaged allies, selects attacks using audited damage and
  retaliation, advances to useful ranges, and ends units/turns explicitly.
- All mutations use battleCommand. No free shots, movement, healing or units.
  This is rebuild AI policy, not native tactical-AI equivalence.
- Four complete mixed-arms battles reach outcomes, including cavalry, light/
  heavy guns and generals, with save/load on every decision. Tests check input
  immutability, side handover, deployment reserves, health bounds, occupancy
  and rally choice. Full suite 75/75 passing.

  touch interface or campaign attack integration yet. Full playable Android
  recreation remains incomplete.

FORT GEOMETRY, HEALTH AND GATES — 2026-11-09
- Traced native campaign fort level at 0x4bfdd3..dc: positive levels increment
  before InitArmyBattle. Level 1/2/3 means tactical code 2/3/4, not 1/2/3.
- Captured 12 original LoadMap cases at widths 17,23,29 and levels 0..3.
  Wall column is activeWidth-6; segment health is 150/200/300. Nine health
  slots are retained, with visible wall cells mapped by floor(row/2).

  intact-wall checks and defender gates at rows 5,7,9. Damage arithmetic and
  shared sections follow disassembly at 0x61ef60 (not yet separately emulated).
- Retreat-distance maps now respect intact walls, defender gates and breaches.
  Tests show attackers cannot retreat through an intact wall but can through
  a breach; defenders can pass their gates. Added offline dependency.
- 79/79 tests pass. Still pending: ranged line of fire across fort walls,

FORT ATTACK ARITHMETIC — 2026-11-09
- Captured 108 native FireOn cases varying troop/artillery attacker, fort code,
  attack direction, intact/breached wall and troop/wall target. Fort crossing,
  category and side effects are explicit harness inputs/stubs; this does not
  establish target legality or geometric intersection correctness.
- Fortified troop damage now applies float32 level factors 0.7/0.6/0.5 for
  attackers crossing an intact wall, with an additional 0.5 for non-artillery.
  Defender fire outward and fire across a breach receive no such reduction.
- Artillery deals wall damage equal to trunc(float32(attack)*float32(0.1))
  for direct bombardment, half that pre-truncation amount for collateral wall
  damage when firing at troops across the wall. Exact rounding retained.
- Tests compare troop losses and legal artillery wall-damage cases to native
  results; illegal non-artillery wall requests are rejected by the rebuild.
  General morale handling remains shared with open-field damage.
- Full suite 80/80 passes. These primitives still need wall intersection,
  target legality, emplaced artillery and battle-command integration before
  siege combat can be exposed to campaign/UI. No full-playability claim.

SIEGE TARGETING AND COMMAND INTEGRATION — 2026-11-09

  Breached cells and defender gates are traversable through movement routing.
- Added adjacent-enemy engagement restriction from native CanFireOn: a unit
  engaged by a standing adjacent foe cannot target a distant unit. Shared
  targeting is used by the AI and view highlights as well as opportunity fire.
- Integration tests run repeated bombardment -> save/load -> breach -> movement,
  and verify sheltered troops/artillery/engagement behavior. 83/83 model tests

PHONE SIEGE CONTROLS — 2026-11-09
- Battle view now distinguishes intact walls, defender gates and breached
  sections. Displays shared section health directly on both affected rows.
- Added shared canBombardWall predicate for UI and command validation, covering
  range, occupancy, spent/moved guns, infantry, engagement and playable tiles.

NAVAL BATTLE MOVEMENT — 2026-11-09
- Added work/rebuild/naval_movement_reference.py, executes native block
  5a3ed8..5a40e5 with controlled RNG and only diagnostic logging stubbed.
  outputs/Native-naval-movement-audit.json records651 cases: all12 ship types,
  damaged/full health, nine intent combinations, RNG boundaries, admirals,

  (currentHull+maxHull)*speed/(2*maxHull); fleet average times10, then multiplied
  by10+admiral medals. Each side gets its own RNG roll modulo combined weights;
  success applies its previously chosen close/hold/open intent. Floor distance
  at0 after both attempts. This differs from slowest-ship world-map movement.
- All651 native cases match weights, distance and exact RNG consumption.
  Movement and decision targeted tests pass. No live battle integration yet.
- Read subsequent native loop: firing range=floor(distance/3)+1; disengage if
  beyond initial maximum internal ship range. Both firepower/counts computed
  before either volley. Hits=min(shooters,opposing ship count); power per hit
  divides fleet firepower by hits; defender damages attacker first, then attacker
  damages defender before cleanup. Loop limit50 at5a4349. These are inspected
  instructions, not yet implementation or captured whole-battle verification.
